```markdown
| Recommendation                                                                 | Classa | Levelb |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------|--------|
| Additional risk decision tests                                                  |        |        |
| After assessing 10-year predicted CVD risk and non-traditional CVD risk modifiers, if a risk-based BP-lowering treatment decision remains uncertain for individuals with elevated BP, measuring CAC score, carotid or femoral plaque using ultrasound, high-sensitivity cardiac troponin or B-type natriuretic peptide biomarkers, or arterial stiffness using pulse wave velocity, may be considered to improve risk stratification among patients with borderline increased 10-year CVD risk (5% to &#x3C;10% risk) after shared decision-making and considering costs. | IIb    | B      |
| Elevated cardiac biomarker levels (specifically high-sensitivity cardiac troponin and B-type natriuretic peptide) are significant and effective risk modifiers. |        |        |
| For patients with elevated BP, the presence of diabetes, familial hypercholesterolaemia, established CVD, moderate or severe CKD, or HMOD confers increased CVD risk. |        |        |
| History of pregnancy complications (gestational diabetes, gestational hypertension, pre-term delivery, pre-eclampsia, one or more stillbirths, and recurrent miscarriage) are sex-specific risk modifiers that should be considered to up-classify individuals with elevated BP and borderline increased 10-year CVD risk (5% to &#x3C;10% risk). | IIa    | B      |
| High-risk ethnicity (e.g. South Asian), family history of premature onset atherosclerotic CVD, socio-economic deprivation, auto-immune inflammatory disorders, HIV, and severe mental illness are risk modifiers shared by both sexes that should be considered to up-classify individuals with elevated BP and borderline increased 10-year CVD risk (5% to &#x3C;10% risk). | IIa    | B      |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. Assess 10-year predicted CVD risk and non-traditional CVD risk modifiers.
- If risk-based BP-lowering treatment decision is uncertain for individuals with elevated BP:
- Measure CAC score.
- Alternatively, measure carotid or femoral plaque using ultrasound.
- Alternatively, measure high-sensitivity cardiac troponin or B-type natriuretic peptide biomarkers.
- Alternatively, assess arterial stiffness using pulse wave velocity.
- If risk stratification is improved, consider shared decision-making and costs.
2. Evaluate elevated cardiac biomarker levels.
- If elevated, consider as significant risk modifiers.
3. For patients with elevated BP:
- Check for presence of diabetes, familial hypercholesterolaemia, established CVD, moderate or severe CKD, or HMOD.
- If present, increased CVD risk is conferred.
4. Assess history of pregnancy complications.
- If complications are present, consider as sex-specific risk modifiers for up-classification.
5. Evaluate high-risk ethnicity and family history.
- If present, consider as shared risk modifiers for up-classification.
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

---

### Bảng biểu

| Điều kiện                                   | Tình trạng bệnh nhân                                                                                     |
|---------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| **Elevated BP**                             | Patient with elevated office SBP 120–139 mmHg or DBP 70–89 mmHg                                        |
| **Moderate-to-severe frailty**             |                                                                                                          |
| **Symptomatic orthostatic hypotension**     |                                                                                                          |
| **Age ≥85 years**                          |                                                                                                          |
| **Established CVD, moderate/severe CKD**   | Patient with T2DM (only) and &#x3C;60 years old                                                              |
| **Calculate SCORE2 or SCORE2-OP**         | (Class I)                                                                                               |
| **Calculate SCORE2- Diabetes**             | (Class IIa)                                                                                             |
| **10-year predicted CVD risk**             | ≥10%                                                                                                    |
| **&#x3C;5%**                                    |                                                                                                          |
| **5% – &#x3C;10%**                              | Consider shared risk modifiers (Class IIa)                                                              |
| **Any of the following:**                   | High-risk ethnicity, Family history of premature onset ASCVD, Socio-economic deprivation, Auto-immune diseases, Severe mental illness, HIV |
| **Consider sex-specific risk modifiers**    | (Class IIa)                                                                                             |
| **Any of the following:**                   | Gestational diabetes, Gestational hypertension, Pre-eclampsia, Pre-term delivery, One or more still births, Recurrent miscarriage |
| **If still uncertain**                      | Shared decision-making informed by risk tools (Class IIb)                                               |
| **Lifestyle measures to reduce BP**         | (Class I)                                                                                               |
| **Any of the following meeting abnormal criteria:** | CAC score, Carotid or femoral plaque, High-sensitivity cardiac troponin, NT-proBNP, Pulse wave velocity |
| **Reassess risk/BP one year later**        | (Class IIa)                                                                                             |
| **Pharmacological treatment for patients with ≥130/80 mmHg** | (Class I)                                                                                               |

### Lưu đồ chẩn đoán/điều trị

[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Bắt đầu**: Xem xét bệnh nhân với huyết áp cao.
2. **Điều kiện**:
- Có bệnh lý đi kèm như:
- Frailty vừa đến nặng
- Hạ huyết áp tư thế triệu chứng
- Tuổi ≥ 85
- Bệnh tim mạch đã được thiết lập, CKD vừa đến nặng, T2DM (chỉ) và &#x3C;60 tuổi
3. **Tính toán SCORE2 hoặc SCORE2-OP**: (Class I)
4. **Tính toán SCORE2 - Đái tháo đường**: (Class IIa)
5. **Dự đoán nguy cơ CVD trong 10 năm**:
- Nếu ≥10%:
- Tiến hành các biện pháp giảm nguy cơ.
- Nếu &#x3C;5% hoặc 5% – &#x3C;10%:
- Cân nhắc các yếu tố rủi ro chia sẻ (Class IIa).
6. **Xem xét các yếu tố rủi ro**:
- Nếu có bất kỳ yếu tố nào sau đây:
- Dân tộc có nguy cơ cao
- Tiền sử gia đình về ASCVD khởi phát sớm
- Thiếu thốn kinh tế
- Bệnh tự miễn
- Bệnh tâm thần nặng
- HIV
7. **Xem xét các yếu tố rủi ro theo giới tính**: (Class IIa)
- Nếu có bất kỳ yếu tố nào sau đây:
- Đái tháo đường thai kỳ
- Tăng huyết áp thai kỳ
- Tiền sản giật
- Sinh non
- Một hoặc nhiều lần sinh chết
- Sẩy thai tái phát
8. **Nếu vẫn không chắc chắn**:
- Thực hiện quyết định chung dựa trên công cụ rủi ro (Class IIb).
9. **Biện pháp lối sống để giảm huyết áp**: (Class I)
10. **Nếu có bất kỳ tiêu chí bất thường nào**:
- CAC score
- Plaque carotid hoặc femoral
- Troponin tim mạch nhạy cảm cao
- NT-proBNP
- Tốc độ sóng mạch
11. **Đánh giá lại nguy cơ/BP sau một năm**: (Class IIa)
12. **Điều trị bằng thuốc cho bệnh nhân với ≥130/80 mmHg**: (Class I)
[END_FLOWCHART]

---

```markdown
| Recommendation                                                                 | Classa | Levelb |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------|--------|
| Opportunistic screening for elevated BP and hypertension should be considered at least every 3 years for adults aged &#x3C; 40 years.236,237 | IIa    | C      |
| Opportunistic screening for elevated BP and hypertension should be considered at least annually for adults aged ≥ 40 years.231,237 | IIa    | C      |
| In individuals with elevated BP who do not currently meet risk thresholds for BP-lowering treatment, a repeat BP measurement and risk assessment within 1 year should be considered. | IIa    | C      |
| In individuals with increased CVD risk where their screening office BP is 120–139/70–89 mmHg, it is recommended to measure BP out of office, using ABPM and/or HBPM or, if not logistically feasible, by making repeated office BP measurements on more than one visit. | I      | B      |
| Where screening office BP is 140–159/90–99 mmHg, it is recommended that the diagnosis of hypertension should be based on out-of-office BP measurement with ABPM and/or HBPM. If these measurements are not logistically or economically feasible, then diagnosis can be made on repeated office BP measurements on more than one visit. | I      | B      |
| Where screening office BP is ≥160/100 mmHg:                                   |        |        |
| • It is recommended that BP 160–179/100–109 mmHg be confirmed as soon as possible (e.g. within 1 month) preferably by either home or ambulatory BP measurements; | I      | C      |
| • It is recommended when BP ≥180/110 mmHg that hypertensive emergency be excluded. | IIa    | C      |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Screening for Hypertension**
- **Type of Screening**
- Systematic Screening
- Individuals invited to measure BP and CVD risk profile.
- Opportunistic Screening
- BP measured during routine check-ups or treatment of conditions.
- Self-Screening and Non-Physician Screening
- Increasingly used.

2. **Confirming the Diagnosis of Hypertension**
- **Initial Screening**
- Systolic BP >160 mmHg and/or Diastolic BP >100 mmHg
- Prompt re-evaluation within days to weeks (preferably &#x3C;1 month).
- Use ABPM or HBPM.
- BP >180/110 mmHg
- Exclude hypertensive emergencies.
- Manage appropriately.

3. **Recommendations for Confirming Hypertension Diagnosis**
- **Increased CVD Risk**
- Office BP 120–139/70–89 mmHg
- Measure out of office (ABPM/HBPM) or repeated office BP.
- **Office BP 140–159/90–99 mmHg**
- Diagnosis based on out-of-office BP measurement.
- **Office BP ≥160/100 mmHg**
- Confirm BP 160–179/100–109 mmHg within 1 month.
- Exclude hypertensive emergency for BP ≥180/110 mmHg.
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

### Bảng biểu

| Non-elevated BP | Elevated BP       | Hypertension       | Hypertension       | Hypertension       |
|------------------|------------------|--------------------|--------------------|--------------------|
| &#x3C;120/70 mmHg     | 120–139/70–89 mmHg | 140–159/90–99 mmHg | 160–179/100–109 mmHg | ≥180/110 mmHg      |

### Sơ đồ chẩn đoán/điều trị

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **High CVD risk conditions**
- SCORE2/SCORE2-OP ≥10%
- SCORE2/SCORE2-OP 5% – &#x3C;10% + risk modifiers
- **≥40 years?**
- N: Opportunistic BP screening at least every 3 years (Class IIa)
- Y: Opportunistic BP screening at least every year (Class IIa)
2. **Confirm BP**
- Preferably with either home or ambulatory BP measurements (Class I)
- **Confirm BP promptly**
- Preferably with either home or ambulatory BP measurements (Class I)
3. **Evaluate for hypertensive emergency** (Class I)
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

---

### Bảng biểu

| Illness Representation | Example Patient Question | Application to a Hypertension Diagnosis Conversation |
|------------------------|-------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------|
| Identity                | What is the disease/illness label and the related symptoms? | The condition where your systolic BP is ≥140 and/or diastolic BP ≥90 mmHg is called hypertension. We classify systolic BP 120–139 or diastolic BP 70–89 mmHg as elevated BP. For most people, this has no noticeable signs or symptoms, therefore, we need to monitor your BP to assess how medications and behavioural changes are working. |
| Control                | Is the illness controllable through medical intervention or behavioural change? | Hypertension can usually be controlled with medication and behavioural changes such as dietary changes and regular physical activity. For some people we need to try a few different options before we get BP under control. |
| Timeline               | Is this an acute or chronic problem? | This is a serious long-term or chronic condition that will require long-term management. This means that it may need to be managed throughout life. |
| Consequences           | What are the physical and psychosocial consequences? | If hypertension is not controlled, then there is a risk of a serious acute cardiovascular disease event such as a stroke or heart attack; however, if it is managed through the right medical intervention and behavioural changes, then this risk can be reduced and the condition will have less consequences for your life. |
| Causes                 | What caused the condition? | Multiple factors contribute to someone developing hypertension. These include both non-modifiable factors (e.g. genetics and age) and modifiable factors (e.g. diet, weight, and physical activity). We are best focusing on those things that we can control to reduce your BP. |

| Treatment Beliefs      | Example Patient Question | Application to a Hypertension Diagnosis Communication |
|------------------------|-------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------|
| Necessity              | To what extent is treatment necessary? | Taking BP-lowering medication every day is necessary to keep your BP under control and to help prevent a more serious health problem developing. Do you think that these medicines will help you? |
| Concerns               | To what extent does treatment cause concern? | Some patients have concerns about taking daily medications throughout their life, e.g. about side effects. Do you have any concerns about taking your BP medications every day? |

### Lưu đồ

[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Bắt đầu**: Đánh giá bệnh nhân có dấu hiệu tăng huyết áp.
2. **Kiểm tra lịch sử y tế**:
- Có dấu hiệu HMOD hoặc bệnh tim mạch, não?
- **Có**: Tiến hành các bước tiếp theo.
- **Không**: Đánh giá các yếu tố khác.
3. **Đánh giá tuân thủ điều trị**:
- Sử dụng phương pháp khách quan để đánh giá tuân thủ (ví dụ: xét nghiệm máu hoặc nước tiểu).
- **Có**: Tiến hành điều trị.
- **Không**: Cần cải thiện giao tiếp giữa bác sĩ và bệnh nhân.
4. **Kiểm tra các yếu tố nguy cơ CVD**:
- Có cần xét nghiệm bổ sung không?
- **Có**: Thực hiện các xét nghiệm cần thiết.
- **Không**: Tiến hành theo dõi và quản lý huyết áp.
5. **Kết thúc**: Đánh giá và điều chỉnh kế hoạch điều trị.
[END_FLOWCHART]

---

```markdown
| Routine Test                                                                                       | Clinical Utility                                                                                     |
|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Fasting blood glucose (and HbA1c if fasting blood glucose is elevated)                            | Assessing CVD risk and comorbidities                                                                |
| Serum lipids: total cholesterol, LDL cholesterol, HDL and non-HDL cholesterol, triglycerides     | Assessing CVD risk                                                                                   |
| Blood sodium and potassium, haemoglobin and/or haematocrit, calcium, and TSH                      | Screening secondary hypertension (primary aldosteronism, Cushing’s disease, polycythaemia, hyperparathyroidism, and hyperthyroidism) |
| Blood creatinine and eGFR; urinalysis and urinary albumin-to-creatinine ratio                    | Assessing CVD risk and HMOD; Guiding treatment choice; Screening secondary hypertension (renoparenchymal and renovascular) |
| 12-lead ECG                                                                                       | Assessing HMOD (left atrial enlargement, left ventricular hypertrophy); Assessing irregular pulse and other comorbidities (AF, previous acute myocardial infarction) |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Initiation**
- Non-adherence definition: Patient does not initiate medication
- **Assessment**: Prescription fill data and self-report
- **Intervention**:
- Communicate benefits/safety of medication
- Alleviate fear of side effects/harm
- Discuss practicalities

2. **Implementation**
- Non-adherence definition: Patient does not take medication as prescribed
- **Assessments**:
- Prescription refill data
- Self-report
- Electronic monitoring
- Chemical adherence testing (blood/urine)
- **Intervention**:
- Reminders
- Encourage habit formation
- Simplify drug regimen

3. **Persistence**
- Non-adherence definition: Patient discontinues medication
- **Assessment**:
- Prescription refill data
- Self-report
- Electronic monitoring
- Chemical adherence testing (blood/urine)
- **Intervention**:
- Motivational counseling on benefits/safety of medication
- Discuss side effects
- Medication reconciliation
- Review practical aspects (cost and ease of access to medication)
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

---

```markdown
| Optional Test                                   | Clinical Utility                                                                                                           |
|------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Echocardiography                                | Assessing HMOD (hypertensive heart disease), established CVD (previous acute myocardial infarction, heart failure), and thoracic aorta dilation |
| CAC by cardiac CT or carotid or femoral artery ultrasound imaging | Assessing HMOD (atherosclerotic plaque)                                                                                  |
| Large artery stiffness (carotid–femoral or brachial–ankle PWV) | Assessing HMOD (arterial stiffness)                                                                                      |
| High-sensitivity cardiac troponin and/or NT-proBNP | Assessing HMOD                                                                                                           |
| Ankle–brachial index                            | Assessing established CVD (lower-extremity arterial disease)                                                              |
| Abdominal ultrasound                            | Assessing established CVD (abdominal aneurysm)                                                                           |
| Fundoscopy                                      | Assessing HMOD (hypertensive retinopathy), diagnosing hypertensive emergency/malignant hypertension (haemorrhages and exudates, papilloedema) |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. Initial Work-up for Hypertension
- Measure blood pressure
- Assess for organ damage
- If organ damage is present, proceed to further evaluation
- If no organ damage, consider lifestyle modifications and follow-up

2. Assessing Hypertension-Mediated Organ Damage (HMOD)
- Perform Echocardiography
- If HMOD is detected, manage accordingly
- Consider additional tests:
- CAC by cardiac CT or ultrasound
- Measure large artery stiffness
- High-sensitivity cardiac troponin and/or NT-proBNP
- Ankle–brachial index
- Abdominal ultrasound
- Fundoscopy

3. If CKD is diagnosed
- Repeat measurements of serum creatinine, eGFR, and urine ACR at least annually
- Consider renal ultrasound and Doppler examination

4. If secondary hypertension is suspected
- Screen for primary aldosteronism, Cushing’s syndrome, and phaeochromocytoma

5. Follow-up and management based on findings
- Adjust treatment as necessary
- Monitor for cardiovascular events
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

---

```markdown
| Recommendations                                                                 | Classa | Levelb |
|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------|--------|
| Echocardiography is recommended in patients with hypertension and ECG abnormalities, or signs or symptoms of cardiac disease. | I      | B      |
| Coronary artery calcium scoring may be considered in patients with elevated BP or hypertension when it is likely to change patient management. | IIb    | B      |
| Echocardiography may be considered in patients with elevated BP, particularly when it is likely to change patient management. | IIb    | B      |
| Measurement of PWV may be considered in patients with elevated BP or hypertension when it is likely to change patient management. | IIb    | B      |
| Genetic testing should be considered in specialist centres for patients suspected to have rare monogenic causes of secondary hypertension or for those with phaeochromocytoma/paraganglioma. | IIa    | B      |
| Routine genetic testing for hypertension is not recommended.                     | III    | C      |
| Fundoscopy is recommended if BP >180/110 mmHg in the work-up of hypertensive emergency and malignant hypertension, as well as in hypertensive patients with diabetes. | I      | C      |
| Fundoscopy for detecting hypertensive retinopathy may be considered in patients with elevated BP or hypertension. | IIb    | B      |
| Ultrasound examination of the carotid or femoral arteries for detecting plaque may be considered in patients with elevated BP or hypertension when it is likely to change patient management. | IIb    | B      |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. Patient presents with hypertension.
- If ECG abnormalities or signs/symptoms of cardiac disease:
- Perform echocardiography (Recommendation I, Level B).
- If elevated BP:
- Consider coronary artery calcium scoring (Recommendation IIb, Level B).
- Consider echocardiography if likely to change management (Recommendation IIb, Level B).
- Consider measurement of PWV if likely to change management (Recommendation IIb, Level B).
- Consider genetic testing for rare monogenic causes if high prior probability (Recommendation IIa, Level B).
- Routine genetic testing not recommended (Recommendation III, Level C).
- If BP >180/110 mmHg:
- Perform fundoscopy for hypertensive emergency (Recommendation I, Level C).
- If elevated BP:
- Consider fundoscopy for hypertensive retinopathy (Recommendation IIb, Level B).
- Consider ultrasound of carotid/femoral arteries for plaque detection if likely to change management (Recommendation IIb, Level B).
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

### Bảng biểu

| Tổ chức | Thông số | Giá trị | Ghi chú |
|---------|----------|---------|---------|
| Thận   | eGFR     | &#x3C;60 mL/min/1.73 m² | Bất kể albuminuria |
|         | ACR      | ≥30 mg/g | Bất kể eGFR |
|         |          |         |         |
| Tim     | LVH      | >35 mm  |         |
|         | ECG      | Sokolow–Lyon: SV1+RV5 RaVL ≥11 mm |         |
|         |          | Cornell voltage: SV3+RaVL>28 mm (nam) SV3+RaVL>20 mm (nữ) |         |
|         |          | LV mass/height².7(g/m².7): >50 (nam) >47 (nữ) |         |
|         |          | LV mass/BSA(g/m²): >115 (nam) >95 (nữ) |         |
|         |          | LV concentric geometry: RWT ≥0.43 |         |
|         |          |         |         |
| Echocardiography | Diastolic dysfunction | LA volume/height² (mL/m²): >18.5 (nam) >16.5 (nữ) |         |
|         |          | LA volume index (mL/m²): 34 |         |
|         |          | e’ &#x3C;7cm; E/e’ >14 |         |
|         |          |         |         |
| Biomarkers | hs-cTnT or I | >99th percentile upper reference limit | Đối với người &#x3C;40 tuổi |
|         | NT-proBNP | >125 pg/mL nếu &#x3C;75 tuổi hoặc >450 pg/mL nếu ≥75 tuổi |         |
|         |          |         |         |
| Carotid or femoral arteries | Plaque | Focal wall thickening >1.5 mm | Siêu âm |
|         | Pulse wave velocity | Carotid-femoral PWV >10 m/s |         |
|         |          | Brachial-ankle PWV >14 m/s |         |
|         |          |         |         |
| Cardiac CT | Coronary artery calcium score | >100 Agatston units |         |

### Lưu đồ chẩn đoán/điều trị

[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Bắt đầu**: Đánh giá bệnh nhân có huyết áp cao.
2. **Đo lường**:
- **Thận**: Kiểm tra eGFR và ACR.
- Nếu eGFR &#x3C;60 mL/min/1.73 m² hoặc ACR ≥30 mg/g, xác định có bệnh thận vừa đến nặng.
3. **Tim**:
- Kiểm tra LVH qua ECG.
- Nếu LVH >35 mm, tiếp tục đánh giá LV mass và hình dạng.
4. **Echocardiography**:
- Đánh giá chức năng tâm trương.
- Nếu có rối loạn chức năng tâm trương, kiểm tra LA volume và chỉ số LA volume.
5. **Biomarkers**:
- Đối với bệnh nhân &#x3C;40 tuổi có huyết áp cao, kiểm tra hs-cTnT và NT-proBNP.
6. **Siêu âm động mạch**:
- Kiểm tra sự hiện diện của mảng bám.
- Nếu có mảng bám, tiếp tục đánh giá PWV.
7. **Cardiac CT**:
- Kiểm tra điểm calcium động mạch vành.
- Nếu điểm >100 Agatston units, xác định có tổn thương cơ quan do huyết áp.
8. **Kết thúc**: Đưa ra quyết định điều trị dựa trên kết quả.
[END_FLOWCHART]

```markdown
| **Definition of Resistant Hypertension**                                                                                     |
|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Hypertension is defined as resistant when a treatment strategy including appropriate lifestyle measures and treatment with maximum or maximally tolerated doses of a diuretic (thiazide or thiazide-like), a RAS blocker, and a calcium channel blocker fail to lower office systolic and diastolic BP values to &#x3C;140 mmHg and/or &#x3C;90 mmHg, respectively. These uncontrolled BP values must be confirmed by out-of-office BP measurements (HBPM or ABPM—Section 5.1 for relevant BP thresholds). |
| **Key Considerations**                                                                                                        |
| - Resistant hypertension is not a disease, but an indicator that should be used to identify patients at high risk for CVD, in which secondary hypertension is also frequent. |
| - Pseudo-resistant hypertension must be excluded, including that caused by non-adherence to treatment.                        |
| - In patients with decreased eGFR (i.e. &#x3C; 30 mL/min/1.73 m²) an adequately up-titrated loop diuretic is necessary to define resistant hypertension. |
| - Patients with suspected resistant hypertension should be referred to specialized centres.                                    |
| - These ESC Guidelines do not include the terms ‘controlled resistant hypertension’ (BP at target but requiring ≥ 4 medications) or ‘refractory hypertension’ (BP not at target despite ≥ 5 medications). |
| **ABPM, HBPM, RAS, eGFR**                                                                                                     |
| ABPM, ambulatory blood pressure monitoring; BP, blood pressure; CVD, cardiovascular disease; eGFR, estimated glomerular filtration rate; HBPM, home blood pressure monitoring; RAS, renin–angiotensin system. |
| **Table 11: Conditions Found to Cause Pseudo-Resistance or Resistance to Blood Pressure-Lowering Treatment**                 |
| **Causes of Pseudo-Resistance Hypertension**                                                                                  |
| - Poor adherence to and persistence with treatment                                                                             |
| - White-coat phenomenon                                                                                                        |
| - Poor BP measurement method                                                                                                   |
| - Marked brachial artery calcification (Osler phenomenon)                                                                     |
| - Clinician inertia (inadequate doses, inappropriate combinations of BP-lowering drugs)                                       |
| - Munchausen syndrome (rare)                                                                                                   |
| **Causes of Resistant Hypertension**                                                                                          |
| - Behavioural factors                                                                                                          |
| - Overweight/obesity                                                                                                           |
| - Physical inactivity                                                                                                           |
| - Excess daily dietary sodium                                                                                                   |
| - Excess habitual alcohol consumption                                                                                            |
| - Use of drugs or substances that may increase BP                                                                             |
| - Undetected secondary hypertension                                                                                             |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Start**
2. **Patient presents with hypertension**
- Is the BP controlled with treatment?
- Yes: **Consider as controlled resistant hypertension** (BP at target but requiring ≥ 4 medications)
- No: **Proceed to next step**
3. **Confirm BP values**
- Are BP values &#x3C;140 mmHg and &#x3C;90 mmHg?
- Yes: **Consider as resistant hypertension**
- No: **Reassess treatment**
4. **Exclude pseudo-resistance**
- Is there poor adherence to treatment?
- Yes: **Address adherence issues**
- No: **Proceed to next step**
5. **Evaluate secondary causes**
- Is there evidence of secondary hypertension?
- Yes: **Investigate further**
- No: **Consider as primary resistant hypertension**
6. **Refer to specialized centre for further testing**
7. **End**
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

---

```markdown
| Factor                                   | Effect on plasma aldosterone levels | Effect on renin levels | Effect on ARR      |
|------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------|-----------------------|---------------------|
| Serum potassium status                   |                                     |                       |                     |
| Hypokalaemia                             | ↓                                   | →↑                    | ↓ (FN)              |
| Potassium loading                        | ↑                                   | →↓                    | ↑                   |
| Sodium restriction                       | ↑                                   | ↑↑                    | ↓ (FN)              |
| Sodium loading                           | ↓                                   | ↓↓                    | ↑ (FP)              |
| Drugs                                    |                                     |                       |                     |
| Beta-adrenergic blockers                 | ↓                                   | ↓↓                    | ↑ (FP)              |
| Calcium channel blockers (DHPs)         | →↓                                  | →↑                    | →↓ (FN with short-acting DHPs) |
| ACE inhibitors                           | ↓                                   | ↑↑                    | ↓ (FN)              |
| ARBs                                     | ↓                                   | ↑↑                    | ↓ (FN)              |
| Potassium-sparing diuretics             | ↑                                   | ↑↑                    | ↓ (FN)              |
| Potassium-wasting diuretics             | →↑                                  | ↑↑                    | ↓ (FN)              |
| Alpha-2 agonists (clonidine, methyldopa)| ↓                                   | ↓↓                    | ↑ (FP)              |
| NSAIDs                                   | ↓                                   | ↓↓                    | ↑ (FP)              |
| Steroids                                 | ↓                                   | →↓                    | ↑ (FP)              |
| Contraceptive agents (drospirenone)     | ↑                                   | ↑                     | ↑ (FP)              |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. Assess Renin and Aldosterone Status
- Discontinue interfering drugs (e.g., beta-blockers, RAS blockers, diuretics)
- Use long-acting CCBs or alpha-receptor antagonists if needed
- If MRAs cannot be stopped, note that ARR testing accuracy is marginally impacted

2. Assess Sodium Intake
- Preferably 24h urinary sodium or sodium-to-creatinine ratio
- Consider menstrual cycle timing for females

3. Evaluate for Obstructive Sleep Apnoea Syndrome (OSAS)
- Suspect in patients with resistant hypertension and suggestive symptoms
- Use validated questionnaires to identify high-risk patients
- Confirm diagnosis with simplified polysomnogram (AHI > 5)

4. Assess for Renovascular Hypertension (RVH)
- Imaging tests: renal artery Doppler ultrasound, abdominal CT angiography, or MRI
- Consider bystander renal artery stenosis in essential hypertension cases
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

### Bảng 13: Các xét nghiệm tùy chọn nên được sử dụng để sàng lọc tăng huyết áp thứ phát trong trường hợp có dấu hiệu, triệu chứng hoặc tiền sử bệnh lý gợi ý

| Nguyên nhân của tăng huyết áp thứ phát          | Xét nghiệm sàng lọc                                                                                     |
|-------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| **Primary aldosteronism**                        | Aldosterone-to-renin ratio
Thông tin hữu ích cũng có thể được cung cấp bằng cách xem xét các mức kali trước đó (hạ kali máu làm tăng khả năng có đồng mắc tăng aldosteron nguyên phát) |
| **Renovascular hypertension**                    | Siêu âm doppler thận
CT động mạch bụng hoặc MRI                                                   |
| **Phaeochromocytoma/paraganglioma**            | 24 giờ nước tiểu và/hoặc metanephrine và normetanephrine huyết tương                                  |
| **Obstructive sleep apnoea syndrome**           | Polysomnography di động qua đêm                                                                         |
| **Renal parenchymal disease**                    | Creatinine, natri và kali huyết tương
eGFR
Que thử nước tiểu cho máu và protein
Tỷ lệ albumin-creatinine trong nước tiểu
Siêu âm thận |
| **Cushing’s syndrome**                           | Cortisol tự do 24 giờ trong nước tiểu
Xét nghiệm ức chế dexamethasone liều thấp                 |
| **Thyroid disease (hyper- or hypothyroidism)**  | TSH                                                                                                     |
| **Hyperparathyroidism**                          | Hormone cận giáp
Canxi và phosphate                                                                |
| **Coarctation of the aorta**                     | Siêu âm tim
CT động mạch chủ                                                                        |

CT, computed tomography; eGFR, estimated glomerular filtration rate; MRI, magnetic resonance imaging; TSH, thyroid-stimulating hormone.

### Bảng biểu

| **Renovascular Hypertension** | **Signs and Symptoms** | **Pathophysiology** | **Diagnosis** | **Treatment** |
|------------------------------|------------------------|---------------------|---------------|----------------|
|                              | Migraine, pulsatile tinnitus (FMD) | Significant renal artery stenosis: | Renal doppler ultrasound | Medical: optimal CV risk management |
|                              | Vascular bruits        | Atherosclerosis      | Abdominal CT-Angio or MRI | Interventional: renal angioplasty without (FMD) or with stenting (atherosclerosis) |
|                              | Arterial dissections and/or aneurysms (FMD) | Fibromuscular dysplasia | | |
|                              | Pulmonary oedema (bilateral) | Rare causes         | | |
|                              | Multisite atherosclerosis | | | |
|                              | Unexplained small kidney or kidney asymmetry | | | |
|                              | GFR, albuminuria, renin | | | |
|                              | Acute eGFR after RAS blocker | | | |
|                              | Age &#x3C;40 years (FMD)   | | | |
|                              | Age >60 years with acute change in BP or flash pulmonary oedema (atherosclerosis) | | | |

| **Obstructive Sleep Apnoea** | **Signs and Symptoms** | **Pathophysiology** | **Diagnosis** | **Treatment** |
|-------------------------------|------------------------|---------------------|---------------|----------------|
|                               | Restless/intermittent sleep, recurrent awakenings | Intermittent upper airway obstruction during sleep | Overnight ambulatory polysomnography | Weight loss |
|                               | Daytime sleepiness, fatigue, impaired concentration | | | CPAP |
|                               | Apnoea, snoring       | | | Mandibular advancement devices |
|                               | Increased neck circumference | | | |

### Lưu đồ chẩn đoán/điều trị

[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Renovascular Hypertension**
- **Signs and Symptoms**
- Migraine, pulsatile tinnitus (FMD)
- Vascular bruits
- Arterial dissections and/or aneurysms (FMD)
- Pulmonary oedema (bilateral)
- Multisite atherosclerosis
- Unexplained small kidney or kidney asymmetry
- GFR, albuminuria, renin
- Acute eGFR after RAS blocker
- Age &#x3C;40 years (FMD)
- Age >60 years with acute change in BP or flash pulmonary oedema (atherosclerosis)
- **Diagnosis**
- Renal doppler ultrasound
- Abdominal CT-Angio or MRI
- **Treatment**
- Medical: optimal CV risk management
- Interventional: renal angioplasty without (FMD) or with stenting (atherosclerosis)

2. **Obstructive Sleep Apnoea**
- **Signs and Symptoms**
- Restless/intermittent sleep, recurrent awakenings
- Daytime sleepiness, fatigue, impaired concentration
- Apnoea, snoring
- Increased neck circumference
- **Pathophysiology**
- Intermittent upper airway obstruction during sleep
- **Diagnosis**
- Overnight ambulatory polysomnography
- **Treatment**
- Weight loss
- CPAP
- Mandibular advancement devices
[END_FLOWCHART]

```markdown
| Recommendations                                                                 | Classa | Levelb |
|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------|--------|
| It is recommended that patients with hypertension presenting with suggestive signs, symptoms or medical history of secondary hypertension are appropriately screened for secondary hypertension. | I      | B      |
| Screening for primary aldosteronism by renin and aldosterone measurements should be considered in all adults with confirmed hypertension (BP ≥ 140/90 mmHg). | IIa    | B      |
| Opportunistic screening with office BP measurements to monitor development of BP during late childhood and adolescence, especially if one or both parents have hypertension, should be considered to better predict development of adult hypertension and associated CVD risk. | IIa    | B      |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Patient presents with hypertension**
- Check for suggestive signs, symptoms, or medical history of secondary hypertension.
- If present, proceed to screening for secondary hypertension.
- If not present, consider lifestyle interventions.

2. **Screening for secondary hypertension**
- Conduct appropriate tests.
- If secondary hypertension is confirmed, initiate treatment based on the underlying cause.

3. **Screening for primary aldosteronism**
- Measure renin and aldosterone in all adults with confirmed hypertension (BP ≥ 140/90 mmHg).
- If primary aldosteronism is suspected, proceed with further diagnostic testing.

4. **Opportunistic screening in children/adolescents**
- Monitor BP during late childhood and adolescence, especially if there is a family history of hypertension.
- If hypertension is detected, consider early intervention strategies.

5. **Lifestyle interventions**
- Recommend dietary changes, physical activity, and other lifestyle modifications to reduce BP and CVD risk.
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

---

```markdown
| Exercise Type                          | Duration/Intensity                                      | Recommendation       |
|----------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------|
| Aerobic exercise training               | At least 150 min/week moderate-intensity or            | Class I               |
|                                        | 75 min/week vigorous intensity: brisk walking, jogging, |                       |
|                                        | cycling, swimming                                       |                       |
| Increase daily physical activity        | Steps/day, take stairs, walk/cycle                     |                       |
| Avoid sedentary lifestyle              |                                                        |                       |
| Isometric resistance exercise training   | Low-to-moderate-intensity (3 sets of 1–2 min contraction: hand-grip, plank, wall sit) |                       |
| Dynamic or isometric resistance training | 2–3 times/week                                         | Class I               |
| Dynamic resistance exercise training    | Large muscle groups, low-to-moderate-intensity (2–3 sets with 10–15 reps: squat, push-ups, sit-up) |                       |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. Start
2. Assess physical activity level
- If &#x3C;150 min/week moderate-intensity or &#x3C;75 min/week vigorous intensity
- Recommend aerobic exercise training
- Brisk walking, jogging, cycling, swimming
- If >150 min/week moderate-intensity or >75 min/week vigorous intensity
- Continue current regimen
3. Increase daily physical activity
- Steps/day, take stairs, walk/cycle
4. Avoid sedentary lifestyle
5. Assess resistance training needs
- If low-to-moderate-intensity resistance training not included
- Recommend isometric resistance exercise training
- 3 sets of 1–2 min contraction: hand-grip, plank, wall sit
- If resistance training included
- Recommend dynamic or isometric resistance training
- 2–3 times/week
6. Assess dietary sodium and potassium intake
- If sodium intake >2 g/day
- Recommend sodium restriction
- If potassium intake &#x3C;3.5 g/day
- Recommend increasing potassium intake
7. End
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

| Giảm lượng rượu                            | Giảm huyết áp và giảm nguy cơ tim mạch                                                       |
| Không hút thuốc                            | Giảm nguy cơ tim mạch (không ảnh hưởng đến huyết áp)                                        |

### Lưu đồ

[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Bắt đầu**
- Xác định tình trạng huyết áp
2. **Huyết áp cao?**
- Có: Tiến hành các bước tiếp theo
- Không: Theo dõi định kỳ
3. **Tăng cường hoạt động thể chất**
- Thực hiện aerobic (bài tập sức bền)
- Theo dõi huyết áp
4. **Tăng cường lượng kali**
- Tăng lượng kali từ thực phẩm hoặc bổ sung
- Theo dõi mức kali trong máu
5. **Tối ưu hóa quản lý cân nặng và chế độ ăn**
- Điều chỉnh chế độ ăn uống
- Theo dõi huyết áp
6. **Giảm lượng muối**
- Giảm natri clorua trong chế độ ăn
- Theo dõi huyết áp
7. **Giảm lượng rượu**
- Hạn chế tiêu thụ rượu
- Theo dõi huyết áp
8. **Không hút thuốc**
- Khuyến khích bỏ thuốc lá
- Theo dõi nguy cơ tim mạch
9. **Kết thúc**
- Đánh giá lại tình trạng huyết áp và nguy cơ tim mạch
[END_FLOWCHART]

```markdown
| **Topic**                                   | **Details**                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
|---------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| High-Intensity Interval Training            | Elicits comparable BP reductions to moderate continuous exercise, with a slight reduction of 2.6 mmHg in systolic BP.                                                                                                         |
| Physical Activity and Hypertension          | Engaging in physical activity is associated with reduced CVD mortality risk in patients with known hypertension.                                                                                                            |
| GLP-1 Receptor Agonists                     | Medications like semaglutide can lead to significant weight reduction and BP reduction in patients with obesity.                                                                                                             |
| Alcohol Consumption                         | Short-term effects of alcohol on BP are dose-dependent; low-dose alcohol (&#x3C;14 g) does not affect BP, while medium-dose (14–28 g) decreases BP, and high-dose (>30 g) first decreases BP then increases it.                     |
| Coffee and Hypertension                     | Coffee intake is not associated with a higher risk of hypertension; higher consumption may be linked to a lower risk.                                                                                                         |
| Weight Reduction and Diet                   | An average weight loss of 5 kg is associated with reductions in systolic and diastolic BP. Maintaining a weight loss of 5%–10% can improve BP, glucose, and lipid metabolism.                                                |
| Smoking and CVD                            | Smoking cessation is one of the most effective measures to prevent major CVD events. Smoking affects ambulatory BP by raising daily BP.                                                                                       |
| DASH Diet                                   | Evidence-based diets like the DASH diet are effective in reducing BP and CVD risk.                                                                                                                                           |
| E-Cigarettes                                | The effects of e-cigarettes on BP remain unclear, but there is evidence suggesting they can increase BP.                                                                                                                     |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Start**
2. **Assess Patient's Condition**
- Known Hypertension?
- Yes: Proceed to Physical Activity Recommendations
- No: Proceed to General Health Recommendations
3. **Physical Activity Recommendations**
- Engage in moderate-intensity aerobic exercise (≥150 min/week)
- Alternatively, engage in vigorous-intensity exercise (≥75 min/week)
- Consider resistance training (2-3 times/week)
4. **Monitor BP Response to Exercise**
- Exaggerated BP response?
- Yes: Consider further evaluation for hypertension
- No: Continue with exercise regimen
5. **Dietary Recommendations**
- Implement weight reduction strategies
- Follow DASH diet or Mediterranean diet
- Limit alcohol and sugar-sweetened beverage intake
6. **Smoking Cessation**
- Advise on smoking cessation
- Provide support for quitting
7. **Follow-Up**
- Regularly monitor BP and adjust recommendations as necessary
8. **End**
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

---

```markdown
| Recommendations                                                                 | Classa | Levelb |
|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------|--------|
| Restriction of sodium to approximately 2 g per day is recommended where possible in all adults with elevated BP and hypertension [this is equivalent to about 5 g of salt (sodium chloride) per day or about a teaspoon or less]. | I      | A      |
| Moderate intensity aerobic exercise of ≥150 min/week (≥30 min, 5–7 days/week) or alternatively 75 min of vigorous intensity aerobic exercise per week over 3 days are recommended and should be complemented with low- or moderate-intensity dynamic or isometric resistance training (2–3 times/week) to reduce BP and CVD risk. | I      | A      |
| It is recommended to aim for a stable and healthy BMI (e.g. 20–25 kg/m²) and waist circumference values (e.g. &#x3C; 94 cm in men and &#x3C; 80 cm in women) to reduce BP and CVD risk. | I      | A      |
| Adopting a healthy and balanced diet, such as the Mediterranean or DASH diets, is recommended to help reduce BP and CVD risk. | I      | A      |
| Men and women are recommended to drink less alcohol than the upper limit, which is about 100 g/week of pure alcohol. | I      | B      |
| It is recommended to restrict free sugar consumption, in particular sugar-sweetened beverages, to a maximum of 10% of energy intake. | I      | B      |
| It is recommended to stop tobacco smoking, initiate supportive care and refer to smoking cessation programmes, as tobacco use strongly and independently causes CVD, CVD events, and all-cause mortality. | I      | A      |
| In patients with hypertension without moderate to advanced CKD and with high daily sodium intake, an increase of potassium intake by 0.5–1.0 g/day should be considered. | IIa    | A      |
| In patients with CKD or taking potassium-sparing medication, monitoring serum levels of potassium should be considered if dietary potassium is being increased. | IIa    | C      |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. Start
2. Assess Blood Pressure (BP)
- If BP is elevated:
- Recommend sodium restriction to ~2 g/day
- Recommend moderate intensity aerobic exercise (≥150 min/week)
- Recommend healthy BMI and waist circumference
- Recommend healthy diet (Mediterranean or DASH)
- Recommend alcohol restriction (&#x3C;100 g/week)
- Recommend sugar restriction (&#x3C;10% energy intake)
- Recommend smoking cessation
- If high sodium intake and no CKD:
- Consider potassium increase (0.5–1.0 g/day)
- If CKD or potassium-sparing medication:
- Monitor serum potassium levels
- If BP is not elevated:
- Continue regular monitoring
3. End
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

### Markdown Table

| **Guidelines** | **Details** |
|----------------|-------------|
| ESC Guidelines | 3959 |
| Treatment for Resistant Hypertension | Acknowledges uncertainty of outcomes benefit. Future outcome trials of MRAs, including finerenone, are encouraged in patients with hypertension. |
| Monotherapy Recommendation | Monotherapy is recommended in the first instance. |
| Combination Therapy Caveat | Patient-level response to individual BP-lowering drug classes can be heterogeneous. Caution regarding adverse effects is warranted. |
| Evidence for Combination Therapy | Evidence for reduced CVD outcomes with BP-lowering drugs in combination therapy is based on observational studies. |
| ALLHAT Trial | The alpha-blocker arm was stopped early due to futility of benefit for the CVD outcome. |
| New Therapies | New therapies with BP-lowering properties await supportive evidence from cardiovascular outcomes trials. |
| Drug Classes Recommended | Major drug classes recommended: ACE inhibitors, ARBs, dihydropyridine CCBs, and thiazide-like diuretics. |
| Exception in Combination | The combination of two RAS blockers is not recommended. |
| Single-Pill Combinations | A single-pill containing two major drug classes is recommended initially at low dose. |
| SGLT2 Inhibitors | Showed favorable effects on CVD events and renal haemodynamics. |
| Other New Drugs | Include GLP-1 agonists and non-steroidal MRAs like finerenone. |
| Eplerenone Dosing | Eplerenone may need to be dosed higher (50–200 mg) for effective BP lowering. |
| Beta-Blockers | Preferred when a beta-blocker is chosen, especially if not already indicated. |
| Combination Therapy Efficacy | Combining drugs from different classes can lead to greater BP reduction. |
| Polypills | Effective in more general CVD prevention but not available for routine clinical use in many European countries. |
| Algorithm for BP Lowering | Introduces a low-dose and then triple-combination strategy while monitoring tolerance. |

### Flowchart

[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Start Treatment for Resistant Hypertension**
- Acknowledge uncertainty of outcomes benefit.
- Encourage future outcome trials of MRAs.

2. **Initial Treatment**
- **Monotherapy Recommended**
- If ineffective, consider combination therapy.

3. **Combination Therapy Considerations**
- Assess patient-level response to individual BP-lowering drug classes.
- Caution regarding adverse effects.

4. **Evidence Review**
- Review observational studies for CVD outcomes with combination therapy.
- Note ALLHAT trial findings.

5. **New Therapies**
- Consider new therapies with BP-lowering properties.
- Await supportive evidence from cardiovascular outcomes trials.

6. **Drug Class Recommendations**
- Recommend major drug classes: ACE inhibitors, ARBs, dihydropyridine CCBs, thiazide-like diuretics.
- **Exception**: Do not combine two RAS blockers.

7. **Single-Pill Combinations**
- Recommend single-pill containing two major drug classes at low dose.

8. **Consider SGLT2 Inhibitors and Other New Drugs**
- Evaluate effects on CVD events and renal haemodynamics.

9. **Eplerenone Dosing**
- Adjust dosing as necessary for effective BP lowering.

10. **Beta-Blocker Consideration**
- Prefer beta-blockers if not already indicated.

11. **Combination Therapy Efficacy**
- Combine drugs from different classes for greater BP reduction.

12. **Polypills**
- Consider polypills for CVD prevention, noting availability issues.

13. **Algorithm for BP Lowering**
- Introduce low-dose and then triple-combination strategy while monitoring tolerance.
[END_FLOWCHART]

### Bảng biểu

| Điều trị                                   | Loại thuốc                                   | Lớp   | Theo dõi (FU)                     | BP kiểm soát sau 1-3 tháng | Ghi chú                                                                 |
|--------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|-------|-----------------------------------|-----------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Monotherapy ban đầu ưu tiên                | ACEi hoặc ARBs / CCBs / Thuốc lợi tiểu      | Class I | Ít nhất mỗi năm                   | Có                          | Tăng huyết áp (120/70–139/89 mmHg), yếu đuối vừa đến nặng, hạ huyết áp tư thế, tuổi ≥85 |
| Liệu pháp kết hợp liều thấp               | ACEi hoặc ARBs / CCBs / Thuốc lợi tiểu      | Class I | Ít nhất mỗi năm                   | Có                          | Nếu có chỉ định thuyết phục (đau thắt ngực, sau nhồi máu cơ tim, suy tim tâm thu, hoặc kiểm soát nhịp tim) |
| Liệu pháp kết hợp liều ba thấp            | ACEi hoặc ARBs / CCBs / Thuốc lợi tiểu      | Class I | Ít nhất mỗi năm                   | Có                          |                                                                         |
| Liệu pháp kết hợp tối đa                  | ACEi hoặc ARBs / CCBs / Thuốc lợi tiểu      | Class I | Ít nhất mỗi năm                   | Có                          |                                                                         |
| Tăng huyết áp kháng trị rõ ràng           | Chuyển đến phòng khám tăng huyết áp         | Class IIa |                                   |                             | Kiểm tra tuân thủ                                                       |
|                                            | Thêm spironolactone                         | Class IIa |                                   |                             | Xem phần quản lý tăng huyết áp kháng trị để biết thêm bước cần thiết |

### Lưu đồ

[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Bắt đầu**: Tăng huyết áp (120/70–139/89 mmHg)
2. **Kiểm tra tình trạng**:
- Nếu có yếu đuối vừa đến nặng, hạ huyết áp tư thế, tuổi ≥85
- **Điều trị**: Monotherapy ban đầu (ACEi hoặc ARBs / CCBs / Thuốc lợi tiểu)
- **Theo dõi**: Ít nhất mỗi năm
- **Kiểm tra BP**: Sau 1-3 tháng
- Nếu BP kiểm soát: Tiếp tục theo dõi
- Nếu không: Chuyển sang bước tiếp theo
3. **Liệu pháp kết hợp**:
- **Liều thấp**: Thêm thuốc (ACEi hoặc ARBs / CCBs / Thuốc lợi tiểu)
- **Theo dõi**: Ít nhất mỗi năm
- **Kiểm tra BP**: Sau 1-3 tháng
- Nếu BP kiểm soát: Tiếp tục theo dõi
- Nếu không: Chuyển sang bước tiếp theo
4. **Liệu pháp kết hợp liều ba thấp**:
- **Điều trị**: Thêm beta-blockers nếu có chỉ định thuyết phục
- **Theo dõi**: Ít nhất mỗi năm
- **Kiểm tra BP**: Sau 1-3 tháng
5. **Liệu pháp kết hợp tối đa**:
- **Điều trị**: ACEi hoặc ARBs / CCBs / Thuốc lợi tiểu
- **Theo dõi**: Ít nhất mỗi năm
- **Kiểm tra BP**: Sau 1-3 tháng
6. **Tăng huyết áp kháng trị rõ ràng**:
- **Chuyển đến phòng khám tăng huyết áp**
- **Kiểm tra tuân thủ**
- **Thêm spironolactone**
- **Xem phần quản lý tăng huyết áp kháng trị để biết thêm bước cần thiết**
[END_FLOWCHART]

---

### Bảng biểu

| Phân loại huyết áp                     | Giá trị huyết áp                          |
|----------------------------------------|------------------------------------------|
| **Không tăng huyết áp**                | Office SBP &#x3C;120 mmHg và DBP &#x3C;70 mmHg   |
| **Huyết áp tăng nhẹ**                  | Office SBP 120–139 mmHg và/hoặc DBP 70–89 mmHg |
| **Tăng huyết áp**                      | Office SBP ≥140 mmHg và/hoặc DBP ≥90 mmHg |

| Nhóm tuổi và tình trạng                | Hành động                                 | |
|----------------------------------------|------------------------------------------|---|
| Tuổi ≥85, yếu kém vừa đến nặng, OH có triệu chứng, tuổi thọ ngắn | Y | |
| Tuổi &#x3C;40, tăng huyết áp kháng trị, dấu hiệu và triệu chứng gợi ý | N | |
| CVD đã được xác lập, CKD vừa đến nặng, tổn thương cơ quan do tăng huyết áp, tiểu đường, tăng cholesterol gia đình | N | |
| Tính toán SCORE2/SCORE2-OP            | N | |
| &#x3C;5%                                    | 5% – &#x3C;10%                                | ≥10% |
| Đánh giá các yếu tố nguy cơ           | Lifestyle measures                        | |
|                                        | Consider risk tools                      | |
|                                        | Drug treatment for confirmed BP ≥130/80 mmHg | |

### Sơ đồ/Lưu đồ

[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Screening for hypertension**
- Opportunistic: every 3 years &#x3C;40 years; every year ≥40 years
- Confirming hypertension: HBPM/ABPM or repeated standardized office BP
2. **Patient centred care**
- Non-elevated BP: Office SBP &#x3C;120 mmHg and DBP &#x3C;70 mmHg
- Elevated BP: Office SBP 120–139 mmHg and/or DBP 70–89 mmHg
- Hypertension: Office SBP ≥140 mmHg and/or DBP ≥90 mmHg
3. **Decision Point: Age ≥85 years, moderate-to-severe frailty, symptomatic OH, short life expectancy?**
- Y: Established CVD, moderate-to-severe CKD, hypertension-mediated organ damage, diabetes mellitus, familial hypercholesterolaemia
- N: Age &#x3C;40 years, resistant hypertension, suggestive signs and/or symptoms
4. **Screen for secondary hypertension (Class I)**
- Decision Point: Calculate SCORE2/SCORE2-OP (Class I)
- &#x3C;5%: Assess risk modifiers (Class IIa)
- 5% – &#x3C;10%: Lifestyle measures (Class IIb)
- ≥10%: Drug treatment for confirmed BP ≥130/80 mmHg
5. **Monitor BP and medication tolerance**
- Target SBP 120–129 mmHg (Class I)
- Target DBP 70–79 mmHg (Class IIb)
[END_FLOWCHART]

---

```markdown
| Recommendations                                                                 | Classa | Levelb |
|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------|--------|
| Among all BP-lowering drugs, ACE inhibitors, ARBs, dihydropyridine CCBs, and diuretics (thiazides and thiazide-like drugs such as chlorthalidone and indapamide) have demonstrated the most effective reduction of BP and CVD events, and are therefore recommended as first-line treatments to lower BP. | I      | A      |
| It is recommended that beta-blockers are combined with any of the other major BP-lowering drug classes when there are other compelling indications for their use, e.g. angina, post-myocardial infarction, heart failure with reduced ejection fraction, or for heart rate control. | I      | A      |
| It is recommended to take medications at the most convenient time of day for the patient to establish a habitual pattern of medication taking to improve adherence. | I      | B      |
| Given trial evidence for more effective BP control vs. monotherapy, combination BP-lowering treatment is recommended for most patients with confirmed hypertension (BP ≥140/90 mmHg) as initial therapy. | I      | B      |
| Preferred combinations are a RAS blocker (either an ACE inhibitor or an ARB) with a dihydropyridine CCB or diuretic. | I      | B      |
| Exceptions to consider include patients aged ≥85 years, those with symptomatic orthostatic hypotension, moderate-to-severe frailty, or elevated BP (systolic BP 120–139 mmHg or diastolic BP 70–89 mmHg) with a concomitant indication for treatment. | I      | B      |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Initiate BP Treatment**
- Start with monotherapy for patients with elevated BP and increased CVD risk, or in frail older patients (aged ≥85 years).
- Consider slower up-titration and lower dosing.

2. **Assess BP Control**
- If BP is controlled with monotherapy, continue treatment.
- If BP is not controlled:
- Move to a two-drug combination (e.g., RAS blocker + dihydropyridine CCB + thiazide/thiazide-like diuretic).
- If still uncontrolled, increase to a three-drug combination.

3. **Further Adjustments**
- If BP is not controlled with a three-drug combination:
- Consider adding spironolactone.
- If spironolactone is ineffective or not tolerated, consider eplerenone or other BP-lowering medications (e.g., beta-blocker, centrally acting agent, alpha-blocker, hydralazine, potassium-sparing diuretic).

4. **Lifestyle Interventions**
- Concurrent initiation of lifestyle changes and pharmacological therapy is recommended.
- Emphasize the importance of lifestyle changes to the patient.

5. **Follow-Up**
- Schedule frequent follow-ups (e.g., every 1–3 months) until BP is controlled.
- Aim to control BP within 3 months.

6. **Considerations for Elevated BP**
- For patients with elevated BP (120–139/70–89 mmHg) and low CVD risk (10-year risk &#x3C;10%), recommend lifestyle measures.
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

```markdown
| Risk                   | Assumed all at sufficiently high risk to benefit from pharmacological treatment | (a) All adults with SBP 120–129 mmHg
(b) SBP 130–139 AND 10-year estimated CVD risk &#x3C;10% AND no high-risk conditions or risk modifiers or abnormal risk tool tests | (a) SBP 130–139 AND high-risk conditions (e.g. established CVD, diabetes mellitus, CKD, FH or HMOD)
(b) SBP 130–139 AND 10-year estimated CVD risk ≥10%
(c) SBP 130–139 AND 10-year estimated CVD risk 5% - &#x3C;10% AND risk modifiers or abnormal risk tool tests |
| Treatment              | Lifestyle measures for prevention | Lifestyle measures for treatment
Monitor BP and CVD risk yearly | Lifestyle measures and pharmacological treatment (after 3-month delay).
Monitor BP yearly once treatment control is established |
| Target (mmHg)         | Maintain BP &#x3C;120/70 | Aim BP 120–129/70–79 mmHg | Lifestyle measures and pharmacological treatment (immediate)
Monitor BP yearly once treatment control is established |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Initiate Blood Pressure Treatment**
- Confirm Blood Pressure Category
- Non-elevated BP (&#x3C;120/70)
- **Action**: Lifestyle measures for prevention
- Elevated BP (120/70 to 139/89)
- **Risk Assessment**:
- (a) All adults with SBP 120–129 mmHg
- **Action**: Lifestyle measures for treatment
- (b) SBP 130–139 AND 10-year estimated CVD risk &#x3C;10% AND no high-risk conditions
- **Action**: Monitor BP and CVD risk yearly
```

```markdown
| Section | Description                                                                                                           |
|---------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| 8.5     | Intensity of blood pressure-lowering therapy and ideal treatment targets established up to age 85 years.            |
| 8.5.1   | Expected degree of blood pressure reduction with approved drugs.                                                    |
|         | The magnitude of BP reduction achieved with the main classes of BP-lowering medications (ACE inhibitors, ARBs, etc.) |
|         | is similar overall. BP reduction with standard doses can be expected to be approximately 9/5 mmHg with office BP.   |
|         | Combination therapy can lower office BP by up to 20/11 mmHg.                                                       |
|         | Beta-blockers are not considered first-line due to inferior efficacy in reducing CVD events.                        |
|         | The BP-lowering effect generally increases with the dose administered.                                             |
|         | The maximum effect might take longer to manifest, with advised follow-up after 1–3 months.                         |
| 8.5.2   | The ideal target of blood pressure-lowering treatment.                                                              |
|         | Optimal control of BP translates into CVD risk reduction.                                                          |
|         | The BP threshold to initiate therapy is not necessarily the same as the recommended BP target.                      |
|         | The recommended target of BP-lowering therapy is 120–129/70–79 mmHg, provided treatment is well tolerated.         |
|         | The treatment target may differ based on CVD risk.                                                                 |
|         | The current guidelines emphasize one treatment BP target (120–129/70–79 mmHg) to discourage therapeutic inertia.    |
```

```markdown
[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Start**
- Assess patient's blood pressure (BP).

2. **Is BP ≥ 140/90 mmHg?**
- Yes: Proceed to BP-lowering treatment.
- No: Monitor and reassess.

3. **Initiate BP-lowering therapy**
- Choose appropriate medication class (ACE inhibitors, ARBs, etc.).

4. **Monitor BP response after 1-3 months**
- Is BP reduction adequate?
- Yes: Continue current treatment.
- No: Adjust treatment.

5. **Target BP range**
- Aim for 120–129/70–79 mmHg.
- Is treatment tolerated?
- Yes: Maintain target.
- No: Reassess treatment options.

6. **Consider patient factors**
- Age, frailty, and individual preferences.

7. **End**
[END_FLOWCHART]
```

### Markdown Table

| Category                        | Systolic Blood Pressure (SBP) | Recommendation/Notes                                                                 |
|---------------------------------|-------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Non-elevated SBP                | &#x3C;120 mmHg                     |                                                                                     |
| Elevated SBP                    | 120–139 mmHg                  |                                                                                     |
| Systolic hypertension            | ≥140 mmHg                     |                                                                                     |
| Target SBP                      | &#x3C;140 mmHg                     | Preferably 120–129 mmHg (Class I)                                                  |
| Optimal SBP                     | 120 mmHg                      | If tolerated (Class IIb)                                                            |
| Pre-treatment symptomatic        |                               | Orthostatic hypotension and/or age ≥85 years (Class IIa)                          |
| Clinically significant frailty   |                               | Moderate-to-severe frailty at any age and/or limited predicted lifespan (&#x3C;3 years) |
| On-treatment SBP                | 135 mmHg                      | May be considered reasonable for certain patients                                   |
| BP-lowering treatment target     | 120–129/70–79 mmHg           | May not generalize to patients with specific conditions                              |
| Treatment strategy               | ALARA                         | As low as reasonably achievable                                                      |

### Flowchart

[START_FLOWCHART]
1. Assess for symptoms or signs of hypotension
- If symptoms/signs present: Exclude hypertensive emergency
- If no symptoms/signs: Proceed to next step
2. Determine SBP category
- If SBP &#x3C;120 mmHg: Non-elevated SBP
- If SBP 120–139 mmHg: Elevated SBP
- If SBP ≥140 mmHg: Systolic hypertension
3. Set target SBP
- Target SBP &#x3C;140 mmHg (preferably 120–129 mmHg)
- If target not tolerated or in specific settings (e.g., frailty, limited lifespan): Follow ALARA principle
4. Monitor treatment
- Use accurate out-of-office BP measurements (ABPM, HBPM) before starting treatment
- Adjust treatment based on tolerance and effectiveness
[END_FLOWCHART]

---

### Recommendation Table

| Recommendations                                                                 | Classa | Levelb |
|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------|--------|
| To reduce CVD risk, it is recommended that treated systolic BP values in most adults be targeted to 120–129 mmHg, provided the treatment is well tolerated. | I      | A      |
| In cases where BP-lowering treatment is poorly tolerated and achieving a systolic of 120–129 mmHg is not possible, it is recommended to target a systolic BP level that is ‘as low as reasonably achievable’ (ALARA principle). | I      | A      |
| Because the CVD benefit of an on-treatment systolic BP target of 120–129 mmHg may not generalize to the following specific settings, personalized and more lenient BP targets (e.g. &#x3C; 140 mmHg) should be considered among patients meeting the following criteria: pre-treatment symptomatic orthostatic hypotension, and/or age ≥85 years. | IIa    | C      |
| Because the CVD benefit of an on-treatment systolic BP target of 120–129 mmHg may not generalize to the following specific settings, personalized and more lenient BP targets (e.g. &#x3C; 140/90 mmHg) may be considered among patients meeting the following criteria: clinically significant moderate-to-severe frailty at any age, and/or limited predicted lifespan (&#x3C;3 years). | IIb    | C      |
| In cases where on-treatment systolic BP is at or below target (120–129 mmHg) but diastolic BP is not at target (≥80 mmHg), intensifying BP-lowering treatment to achieve an on-treatment diastolic BP of 70–79 mmHg may be considered to reduce CVD risk. | IIb    | C      |

### Flowchart Summary

[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Start**: Assess patient for hypertension.
2. **Initial BP Measurement**: Measure systolic and diastolic BP.
3. **Is BP ≥ 130/80 mmHg?**
- Yes: Proceed to BP-lowering treatment.
- No: Monitor and reassess.
4. **Treatment Tolerance**: Is the treatment well tolerated?
- Yes: Target systolic BP to 120–129 mmHg.
- No: Target BP to ‘as low as reasonably achievable’ (ALARA principle).
5. **Follow-up**: Is BP controlled and stable?
- Yes: Schedule yearly follow-up for BP and CVD risk factors.
- No: Consider medication adjustments.
6. **Specific Settings**: Does the patient meet criteria for lenient BP targets?
- Yes: Consider personalized BP targets (&#x3C;140 mmHg or &#x3C;140/90 mmHg).
- No: Continue standard treatment.
7. **Diastolic BP Target**: Is diastolic BP &#x3C; 80 mmHg?
- Yes: Intensify treatment to achieve diastolic BP of 70–79 mmHg.
- No: Continue monitoring.
8. **End**: Continue regular follow-up and adjust treatment as necessary.
[END_FLOWCHART]

---

### Markdown Table

| Recommendations                                                                                                         | Classa | Levelb |
|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------|--------|
| To reduce BP, and if performed at a medium-to-high volume centre, catheter-based renal denervation may be considered for resistant hypertension patients who have BP that is uncontrolled despite a three BP-lowering drug combination (including a thiazide or thiazide-like diuretic), and who express a preference to undergo renal denervation after a shared risk-benefit discussion and multidisciplinary assessment. | IIb    | B      |
| To reduce BP, and if performed at a medium-to-high volume centre, catheter-based renal denervation may be considered for patients with both increased CVD risk and uncontrolled hypertension on fewer than three drugs, if they express a preference to undergo renal denervation after a shared risk-benefit discussion and multidisciplinary assessment. | IIb    | A      |
| Due to a lack of adequately powered outcomes trials demonstrating its safety and CVD benefits, renal denervation is not recommended as a first-line BP-lowering intervention for hypertension. | III    | C      |
| Renal denervation is not recommended for treating hypertension in patients with moderate-to-severely impaired renal function (eGFR &#x3C;40 mL/min/1.73 m²) or secondary causes of hypertension, until further evidence becomes available. | III    | C      |

### Flowchart

[START_FLOWCHART]
1. **Start**
2. **Assess Patient for Resistant Hypertension**
- If BP is uncontrolled despite a three-drug combination (including a thiazide or thiazide-like diuretic):
- Proceed to **Consider Catheter-Based Renal Denervation**
- Discuss shared risk-benefit with the patient.
- If patient agrees, perform the procedure.
- If BP is controlled or patient does not express preference:
- **Do Not Proceed**
3. **Evaluate for Increased CVD Risk**
- If patient has increased CVD risk and uncontrolled hypertension on fewer than three drugs:
- Proceed to **Consider Catheter-Based Renal Denervation**
- Discuss shared risk-benefit with the patient.
- If patient agrees, perform the procedure.
- If not:
- **Do Not Proceed**
4. **Assess for Adequate Outcomes Trials**
- If no adequately powered outcomes trials exist:
- **Do Not Recommend Renal Denervation as First-Line Treatment**
5. **Assess Renal Function**
- If eGFR &#x3C;40 mL/min/1.73 m² or secondary causes of hypertension:
- **Do Not Recommend Renal Denervation**
6. **End**
[END_FLOWCHART]
